The author thanks Alice Leonhardt for the beautiful typing of earlier versions (up to 2019) and in later versions the author would like to thank the typist for his work and is also grateful for the generous funding of typing services donated by a person who wishes to remain anonymous. First typed October 18, 2017
Saharon Shelah

Abstract
. For λ \lambda inaccessible, we may consider ( < λ ) (<\lambda) -support iteration of some definable in fact specific ( < λ ) (<\lambda) -complete λ + \lambda^{+} -c.c. forcing notions. But do we have “preservation by restricting to a sub-sequence of the iterated forcing”? To regain it we “correct” the iteration. We prove this for a characteristic case for iterations which holds by “nice” for λ = ℵ 0 . \lambda=\aleph_{0}. This is done generally in a work H. Horowitz. For [ She20 ] we use so called strongly ( < λ + ) (<\lambda^{+}) -directed 𝐦 . {\mathbf{m}}. We could here restrict ourselves to reasonable 𝐦 {\mathbf{m}} (see 2.13 (3)).

Introduction

This work is dedicated to proving a theorem on
(<\lambda)
-support
iterations of
(<\lambda)
-complete “nicely” definable
\lambda^{+}
-c.c. forcing notions for
\lambda
inaccessible. A nicely definable forcing notion can be, for example, random reals forcing (when
\lambda={\aleph_{0}}
). Pedantically, at each stage it is a different forcing notion, but it has the same definition at every step of the iteration.
Assume
{\mathbb{Q}}
is such a definition,
\langle{\mathbb{P}}_{\alpha},\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{\beta}:\alpha\leq\alpha_{*},\beta<\alpha_{*}\rangle
is such an iteration,
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\beta}={\mathbb{Q}}^{\mathbf{V}[{\mathbb{P}}_{\beta}]}
has generic
\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{\beta}
. A question is: assuming
\langle\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{\beta}:\beta<\alpha_{*}\rangle
is generic for
{\mathbb{P}}_{\alpha_{*}}
, and
letting
\beta_{*}
be maximal such that
2\beta_{*}\leq\alpha_{*},
does it follows that also the sequence
\langle\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{2\beta}:\beta
satisfies
2\beta<\alpha_{*}\rangle
is
generic for the iteration
\langle{\mathbb{P}}_{\alpha},\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{\beta}:\alpha\leq\beta_{*},\beta<\beta_{*}\rangle
?

The point is that in the parallel case for
\lambda=\aleph_{0}
so for FS-iterated forcing such a claim is true. In fact, by Judah-Shelah
[
JS88
]
, if
\langle{\mathbb{P}}_{\alpha},\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{\beta}:\alpha\leq\alpha(*),\beta<\alpha(*)\rangle
is FS-iteration of Suslin-c.c.c. forcing notions,
\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{\beta}
with the generic
\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{\beta}\in{}^{\omega}\omega
and for notational transparency, its definition is with no parameter and the function
\zeta:\beta(*)\rightarrow\alpha(*)
is increasing
and
{\mathbb{P}}=\langle{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}_{\alpha},\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}^{\prime}_{\beta}:\alpha\leq\beta(*),\beta<\beta(*)\rangle
is FS iteration,

\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}^{\prime}_{\beta}
defined exactly as
\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbb{Q}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{\zeta(\beta)}
but now in
\mathbf{V}^{{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}_{\beta}}
rather than
\mathbf{V}^{{\mathbb{P}}_{\zeta(\beta)}}
then
\Vdash_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\alpha(*)}}``\langle\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{\zeta(\beta)}:\beta<\beta(*)\rangle
is
generic for
{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}_{\beta(*)}
over
\mathbf{V}"
. For CS iteration of Suslin proper forcing a weaker result holds, see
[
JS88
, §2]
and
[
She04a
]
.

Now this is not clear to us for
(<\lambda)
-support iteration of
(<\lambda)
-strategically complete forcing notions. The solution is
essentially to change the iteration to what we call “corrected iteration”. We use a “quite generic”
(<\lambda)
-support iteration which “includes” the one we like and use the complete sub-forcing it generates. Here we deal with a characteristic case (used in
[
She20
]
). The proof applies also to partial memory iteration. On wide generalization (including the case
\lambda=\aleph_{0}
) and application (for
\lambda=\aleph_{0}
) this is continued in a work of H. Horowitz and the author
[
HS
]
; more fully
[
HS
]
generalizes §1,§2, §3A,§3B, §3D of the present work whereas §3C, §3E, §3F were added later, and §3C is inverse engineering of
[
HS
, 4.2,4.4]
. Our main result is
2.12
, proving that there is “corrected iteration”, i.e. one satisfying the promised property or see
2.11
and more in
2.16
,
2.17
.

The problem arises as follows. In
[
She20
]
it is proved that for
\lambda
inaccessible, consistently
{\rm cov}_{\lambda}({\rm meagre})
, the covering number of the meagre ideal on
\lambda
is strictly smaller
than
{\mathfrak{d}\/}_{\lambda}
, the dominating number. The result here is used there but the editor prefers to separate it.
In §3F we have an alternative proof of the main theorem, for this we noted in some earlier places
what rely on what.

We have two extreme versions of our frameworks, one we call fat, that is, in Definition
1.10
,
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}},t}=[u_{{\mathbf{m}},t}]^{\leq\lambda}
(used in
[
She20
]
). The other is the lean one when the
{\mathscr{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}},t}
are restricted to the leaves (i.e.
t/E_{{\mathbf{m}}}^{\prime}
). This was the original version and is the one continued in Horowitz-Shelah
[
HS
]
.

The interest in having “
{\mathbf{m}}
is strongly
\lambda^{+}
-directed” is that it implies
\Vdash_{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}}
“
\{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{s}:s\in M\}
cofinal in
\left(\Pi_{{\varepsilon}<\lambda}\theta_{{\varepsilon}},<_{J_{\lambda}^{\rm{bd}}}\right)
”, by
1.29
. Now using
{\mathbf{m}}\in{\mathbf{M}}_{\rm{ec}}
(being full and wide) as constructed in §1C, does not give this, e.g. because there may be
t\in L_{{\mathbf{m}}}
above all members of
M_{{\mathbf{m}}}.
This is circumvented in
2.6
by having, on the one hand for cofinaly many
c\in M_{{\mathbf{m}}},{\mathbf{m}}(<c)\in{\mathbf{M}}_{\rm{ec}}
and on the other hand having “
{\mathbf{m}}
is strongly
(<\lambda^{+})
-directed” (see
2.13
(2)). An alternative approach is to restrict ourselves to the fat context.

This work is continued in
[
HS
]
and lately in
[
S+
]
, which in particular sort out when corrected iteration is necessary; we have lecture on this in the Set Theoretic Conference, in Jerusalem, July 2022.

Notation 0.1
.

We try to use standard notation. We use

\theta,\kappa,\lambda,\mu,\chi,
for cardinals and
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\varepsilon,\zeta,\xi
for ordinals. We use
also
i
and
j
as ordinals. We adopt the Cohen convention that
p\leq q
means that
q
gives more information, in forcing notions. The
symbol
\triangleleft
is preserved for “being a proper initial segment”.
Also recall
{}^{B}A=\{f:f
a function from
B
to
A\}
and let
{}^{\alpha>}A=\cup\{{}^{\beta}A:\beta<\alpha\}
, some prefer
{}^{<\alpha}A
, but
{}^{\alpha>}A
is used systematically in the
author’s papers. Lastly,
J^{\text{\rm bd}}_{\lambda}
denotes the
ideal of the bounded subsets of
\lambda
.

Recall from
[
She20
]
:

Definition 0.2
.

Let
\lambda
be inaccessible,
\bar{\theta}=\langle\theta_{\varepsilon}:\varepsilon<\lambda\rangle
be a sequence of regular cardinals
<\lambda
satisfying
\theta_{\varepsilon}>\varepsilon
.

1) We define the forcing notion
{\mathbb{Q}}={\mathbb{Q}}_{\bar{\theta}}
by:

(\alpha)

p\in{\mathbb{Q}}

iff
:

(a)

p=(\eta,f)=(\eta^{p},f^{p})
,

(b)

\eta\in\prod\limits_{\zeta<\varepsilon}\theta_{\zeta}
for some
\varepsilon<\lambda
, (
\eta
is called the trunk of
p
),

(c)

f\in\prod\limits_{\zeta<\lambda}\theta_{\zeta}
,

(d)

\eta\triangleleft f
.

(\beta)

p\leq_{\mathbb{Q}}q
iff:

(a)

\eta^{p}\trianglelefteq\eta^{q}
,

(b)

f^{p}\leq f^{q}
, i.e.
(\forall\varepsilon<\lambda)f^{p}(\varepsilon)\leq f^{q}(\varepsilon)
,

(c)

if
\ell g(\eta^{p})\leq\varepsilon<\ell g(\eta^{q})
then
\eta^{q}(\varepsilon)\in[f^{p}(\varepsilon),\lambda)
, actually follows.

2) The generic is
\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}=\cup\{\eta^{p}:p\in\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{\bar{\theta}}}\}
.

The new forcing defined above is not
\lambda
-complete anymore. By fixing a trunk
\eta
one can define a short increasing sequence of conditions which goes up to some
\theta_{\zeta}
at the
\zeta
-th coordinate and hence has no upper bound in
\prod\limits_{\zeta<{\varepsilon}}\theta_{\zeta}
. However, this forcing is
(<\lambda)
-strategically complete since the COM (= completeness) player can increase the trunk at each move.

Definition 0.6
.

1) We say that a forcing notion

\mathbb{P}
is
\alpha
-strategically complete
when
for each
p\in\mathbb{P}
in the following game
\Game_{\alpha}(p,\mathbb{P})
between the players COM and INC, the player COM has a winning strategy.

A play lasts
\alpha
moves; in the
\beta
-th move, first the player
COM chooses
p_{\beta}\in\mathbb{P}
such that
p\leq_{\mathbb{P}}p_{\beta}

and
\gamma<\beta\Rightarrow q_{\gamma}\leq_{\mathbb{P}}p_{\beta}
and
second the player INC chooses
q_{\beta}\in\mathbb{P}
such that
p_{\beta}\leq_{\mathbb{P}}q_{\beta}
.

The player COM wins a play if he has a legal move for every
\beta<\alpha
.

2) We say that a forcing notion
\mathbb{P}
is
(<\lambda)
-strategically
complete
when
it is
\alpha
-strategically complete for every

\alpha<\lambda
.

Discussion 1.8
.

Concerning the aim of the choice to use
u_{t}
(and
{\mathscr{P}}_{t}
) in
1.5
, note the following.

1) By the partial order we already can get partial memory, so why not simply use only
u_{t}:=\{s:s<_{L}t\}
? After all, the index set is only partially ordered, not necessarily linearly, so these sets can be independent of each other. The reason is that a partial order is transitive, so this simple definition would imply
s\in u_{t}\Rightarrow u_{s}\subseteq u_{t}
which means (by definition) the memory is transitive, but we do not want that to hold in general, (this is central in
[
She00
]
). Here
\bar{u}
is not necessarily transitive, that is,
s\in u_{t}\nRightarrow u_{s}\subseteq u_{t}
. By a partial order we cannot get it.

2) In
[
She04b
]
,
[
Shea
]
we use
{\mathscr{P}}_{t}
’s which are ideals, but here not
necessarily: this helps, but has a price; we are relying on “
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\bar{\theta}}
is close to
being
\lambda
-centered”, i.e. any subset of
\{p\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{\bar{\theta}}:{\rm tr}(p)=\eta\}
of cardinality
<\theta_{\ell g(\eta)}
has a lub in this forcing. But for the fat context we get more than
(<\lambda)
-complete ideal.

3) What is the point of “
{\mathbf{m}}
being neat”? It tells us that in that case it is easy to be an automorphism of
{\mathbf{m}},
see
1.16
(2), we may forget to say we use it.

Explanation 1.9
.

For
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbf{M}
:

(a)

We shall use
L_{\mathbf{m}}
as the index set for the iteration; always a well founded partial order.

(b)

M_{\mathbf{m}}
is the part of the index set we are really interested in, it may be
(\kappa,<)
as in
[
She20
]
.

(c)

The other part in the interesting case is “generic enough
\mathbf{m}
”, more accurately existentially closed enough so that the iteration restricted to
M
will be “stabilized” under further extensions. That is, for every
{\mathbf{m}}\in{\mathbf{M}}
we define an iteration resulting in the forcing
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}
, adding a generic
\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{s}
for
s\in L_{{\mathbf{m}}}
, we are interested in the extension

{\mathbf{m}}

being required to be well founded.

Remark 1.15
.

1) A reader may wonder, e.g.:

(*)

if
\langle{\mathbf{B}}_{\alpha}:\alpha<\alpha_{*}\leq\lambda\rangle
is a sequence of
\lambda
-Borel subsets of
\Pi_{{\varepsilon}<\lambda}\theta_{{\varepsilon}}
which form a partition (in
{\mathbf{V}}
), does they from a partition also in
{\mathbf{V}}^{{\mathbb{P}}}.

In our case as
{\mathbb{P}}
is
\lambda
-strategically complete (see
1.16
(3A)) the answer is obviously yes.

2) Note that in
(*)
we cannot weaken the assumption too much because “if
{\mathbb{P}}
add a new subset to
\theta<\lambda
this certainly faill”. Even
(<\lambda)
-strategically complete is not enough. Why? assume
\lambda
is a Mahlo cardinal
S\subseteq\{\theta<\lambda:\theta
inaccessible
\}
is stationary, such that (for transparency)
\diamond_{S}
holds. We can find
{\mathscr{T}}
such that:

\boxplus
(a)

{\mathscr{T}}
a subtree of
({}^{\lambda}2,\lhd),

(b)

{\mathscr{T}}
with no
\lhd
-maximal nodes,

(c)

if
\delta\in\lambda\setminus S
a limit ordinal,
\eta\in{}^{\delta}2
and
\alpha<\delta\Rightarrow\eta{\restriction}\alpha\in{\mathscr{T}},
then
\eta\in{\mathscr{T}},

(d)

{\mathscr{T}}
has no
\lambda
-branch.

Let
{\mathbf{B}}_{0}=\{\eta\in{}^{\lambda}2:\bigwedge_{\alpha<\lambda}\eta{\restriction}\alpha\in{\mathscr{T}}\}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{1}={}^{\lambda}2.

In
{\mathbf{V}}
those two
\lambda
-Borel sets form a partition: the first is empty and the second is all. The forcing notion
{\mathscr{T}}
add a
\lambda
-branch to
{\mathscr{T}},
hence
({\mathbf{B}}_{0},{\mathbf{B}}_{1})
are no longer disjoint so fail to form a partition of
{}^{\lambda}2.
Lastly, for
\alpha<\lambda
the forcing notion
{\mathscr{T}}
is
\alpha
-strategically complete (just COM choose
p_{\alpha}\in{\mathscr{T}}
of length
>\alpha
).

3) Alternatively, if it suffice to us to have “for
\alpha<\kappa
, COM do not lose in the game of length
\alpha
” let
\lambda
be inaccessible and
S
as above or just such that
\lambda\setminus S
is fat i.e. for every club
E
of
\lambda
and
\alpha<\lambda
there is an increasing continuous
h:\alpha\to E
such that
S\cap{\rm rang}(h)=\emptyset.
Let
{\mathbb{Q}}=\{\eta:\eta\in{}^{\lambda>}\lambda
be increasing continuous with range disjoint to
S
and
\sup({\rm rang}(\eta_{i}))
is not in
S\}.
Let the sequence
\langle\eta_{i}:i<\lambda\rangle
of pairwise
\unlhd
-incomparable be such that
\lg(\eta_{i})\in S
and
(\forall\alpha<\lg(\eta_{i}))[\eta_{i}{\restriction}\alpha\in{\mathbb{Q}}]
and it is dense in
{\mathbb{Q}}.
For
i<\lambda,
let
{\mathbf{B}}_{1+i}
be
\{\nu\in{}^{\lambda}\lambda:\eta_{i}\lhd\nu\},
so closed and
{\mathbf{B}}_{0}=\{\nu\in{}^{\lambda}\lambda:\nu
is not increasing continuous
\},
now
\langle{\mathbf{B}}_{i}:i<\lambda\rangle
is as required.

4) Another avenue is to assume
\aleph_{0}<\theta={\rm cf}(\theta)<\lambda,S_{0}\subseteq\{\delta<\lambda:{\rm cf}(\lambda)<\theta\},S\subseteq\{\delta<\lambda:{\rm cf}(\delta)=\theta
and
S_{0}\cap\delta
is a stationary subset of
\delta\}.
Now let

and for no
\delta\leq\lg(\eta)
we have
\delta\in S
and for some club

of
\delta
do we have

Continue as in
1.15
(3).

5) Note that if in
0.6
(1) we let INC to choose first, then
1.15
(a) does not work whereas in
1.15
(2), (3) this does no matters.

6) Anyhow in

is a

6) Assume that

Remark 1.17
.

What is the use of e.g. (6), (6A)? See
2.12
(A)(b) and
1.18
.

Proof.

We prove all parts simultaneously by induction on
{\rm dp}_{\mathbf{m}}
.

1) For clause
(\alpha)
for each
\mathbf{m}
, using the induction hypothesis and
1.13
(e),
the problem is only when
{\rm dp}_{\mathbf{m}}(t)={\rm dp}_{\mathbf{m}}-1
and use part (5A) proved below (and
1.13
(c)
(\zeta)
). For
clause
(\beta)
use also part (6A) for
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}(<t)}
proved below in
1.13
(c)
(\zeta)
. In both cases the proof of the parts quoted does not rely on
part (1), (but may depend on the induction hypothesis).

2) Recall that
\lambda
is strongly inaccessible. If
p_{\varepsilon}\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}
for
\varepsilon<\lambda^{+}
then we can find by the
\Delta
-system lemma
a set
u
and unbounded
S\subseteq\lambda^{+}
such that
\varepsilon\neq\zeta\in S\Rightarrow{\rm dom}(p_{\varepsilon})\cap{\rm dom}(p_{\zeta})=u
and
\langle{\rm tr}(p_{\varepsilon}(\beta)):\beta\in u\rangle
is the same for all
\varepsilon\in S
. Now
p_{\varepsilon},p_{\zeta}
has a common upper bound for every
\varepsilon,\zeta\in u
, i.e. we
define
r
by:

\bullet

{\rm dom}(r)={\rm dom}(p_{\varepsilon})\cup{\rm dom}(p_{\zeta})
,

\bullet

r(s)=p_{\varepsilon}(s)
is
s\in{\rm dom}(p_{\varepsilon})\backslash{\rm dom}(p_{\zeta})
,

\bullet

r(s)=p_{\zeta}(s)
if
s\in{\rm dom}(p_{\zeta})\backslash{\rm dom}(p_{\varepsilon})
,

\bullet

if
s\in{\rm dom}(p_{\varepsilon})\cap{\rm dom}(p_{\zeta})

then

r(s)=({\rm tr}(p_{\varepsilon}(s)),\max\{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{p_{\varepsilon}(s)},\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{p_{\zeta}(s)}\})
.

3) By (4), the second sentence + (5B) below which use only the induction hypothesis.

3A) We define
p
by:

\bullet

{\rm dom}(p)=\cup\{{\rm dom}(p_{i}):i<\delta\}

\bullet

{\rm tr}(p(s))=\cup\{{\rm tr}(p_{i}(s)):i<\delta

satisfies
s\in{\rm dom}(p_{i})\}

\bullet

\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{p(s)}=\sup\{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{p_{i}(s)}:i<\delta
satisfies
s\in{\rm dom}(p_{i})\}
.

Note that here having to really start with
\langle\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{p_{i}(s),\iota}:\iota<\iota(p_{i}(s))\rangle
and get
\langle\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{p(s),\iota}:\iota<\iota(p(s))\rangle
, see
1.10
(c)
(\gamma)
causes no problem, similarly in the proof of part (2) - just take the union.

3B) Obvious by the definition of
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}
and
1.13
(c), recalling that
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}(<s)}
is
(<\lambda)
-strategically complete, that is part (4) and (5B).

3C) The proof is by induction on
\rm{dp}_{{\mathbf{m}}}
and is splitted in cases:

Case 1
:
{\rm dp}_{\mathbf{m}}
is zero:

So
L_{\mathbf{m}}
is empty.

Case 2
:
{\rm dp}_{\mathbf{m}}=\alpha+1
:

Hence
L_{2}=\{s\in L:{\rm dp}_{\mathbf{m}}(s)=\alpha\}
is non-empty and
letting
L_{1}=L_{\mathbf{m}}\backslash L_{2}
; clearly
s\in L_{1}\Rightarrow{\rm dp}_{\mathbf{m}}(s)<\alpha
, so
{\rm dp}_{\mathbf{m}{\restriction}L_{1}}\leq\alpha
. Let
\zeta_{*}=\sup(\{\ell g({\rm tr}(p(s))+1:s\in{\rm dom}(p)\}\cup\{\zeta+1\})
. Hence applying parts (3)
and (5B) to
\mathbf{m}{\restriction}L_{1}
, i.e. the induction hypothesis we can find
q_{1}
such that
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}{\restriction}L_{1}}\models``p{\restriction}L_{1}\leq q_{1}"
and
[s\in{\rm dom}(q_{1})\Rightarrow\ell g({\rm tr}(q_{1}(s))>\zeta_{*}]
and
q_{1}
forces a value to

\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{p(s)}{\restriction}\zeta_{*}
, call it
\rho_{s}
for
s\in{\rm dom}(p)\cap L_{2}.

Define
q\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}
by
{\rm dom}(q)={\rm dom}(q_{1})\cup(L_{2}\cap{\rm dom}(p)),q{\restriction}L_{1}=q_{1}
and if
s\in L_{2}\cap{\rm dom}(p)
then
q(s)=(\rho_{s},\rho_{s}\char 94\relax(\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{p(s)}{\restriction}[\zeta_{*},\lambda))
, fully
\iota(q(s))=\iota(p(s)),\bar{s}_{q(s),\iota}=\bar{s}_{p(s),\iota}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{q(s),\iota}
is like
{\mathbf{B}}_{p(s),\iota}
only restricting the range to
(\Pi_{{\varepsilon}<\lambda}\theta_{{\varepsilon}})^{{\rm{tr}(q(s))}}

Easily
q
is as required.

Case 3
:
\delta={\rm dp}_{\mathbf{m}}
is a limit ordinal of cofinality
\geq\lambda
:

So
\alpha=\sup\{{\rm dp}_{\mathbf{m}}(s)+1:s\in{\rm dom}(p)\}
is an ordinal
<\delta
and let
L=\{s\in L_{\mathbf{m}}:{\rm dp}_{\mathbf{m}}(s)<\alpha\}
, so
L
is an initial segment of
L_{\mathbf{m}}
and applying the induction hypothesis to
\mathbf{m}{\restriction}L,p
we get
q
as required in
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}{\restriction}L}
hence in
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}
.

Case 4
:
\delta={\rm dp}_{\mathbf{m}}
is a limit ordinal of
cofinality
<\lambda
:

Let
\langle\alpha_{i}:i<{\rm cf}(\delta)\rangle
be increasing continuous with limit
\delta
, let
\alpha_{{\rm cf}(\delta)}=\delta
and for
i\leq{\rm cf}(\delta)
let
L_{i}:=\{s\in L_{\mathbf{m}}:{\rm dp}_{\mathbf{m}}(s)<1+\alpha_{i}\}
.

Now we choose
(p_{i},\zeta_{i})
by induction on
i<{\rm cf}(\delta)
such that:

(a)

p_{i}\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}{\restriction}L_{i}}
,

(b)

{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}{\restriction}L_{i}}\models``(p{\restriction}L_{i})\leq p_{i}

and
p_{j}\leq p_{i}"
when
j<i
,

(c)

if
i
is a limit ordinal then
p_{i}
is gotten from

\langle p_{j}:j<i\rangle
as in part (4),

(d)

if
s\in{\rm dom}(p_{i})
then
\ell g({\rm tr}(p_{i}(s)))\geq\zeta_{i}
,

(e)

\langle\zeta_{j}:j<i\rangle
is an increasing continuous sequence of ordinals
<\lambda
and if
i
is non-limit then
\zeta_{i}
is
>\zeta
and

\geq|{\rm dom}(p)|
and
>\sup(\{\ell g({\rm tr}(p_{j}(s))):j<i
and
s\in p_{j}\}\cup\{\ell g({\rm tr}(p(s))):s\in{\rm dom}(p)\})
.

Using
1.13
and the induction hypothesis this is easy.

4) For transparency assume
\Vdash``\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle y$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle y$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle y$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle y$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}\in\prod\limits_{\varepsilon<\lambda}\theta_{\varepsilon}"
or just

Observation 1.22
.

1) For
p,q\in\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}
we have:
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}\models{\text{`}\text{`}}p\leq q"

iff

{\rm dom}(p)\subseteq{\rm dom}(q)
and
q
is essentially above
p
inside
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}
, (see
1.7
(9) or below).

2) For
p,q\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}
the following conditions are equivalent:

(a)

q\Vdash``p\in\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}}"
, that is
q
is essentially above
p
, see
1.7
(9),

(b)

if
s\in{\rm dom}(p)

then
either
s\in{\rm dom}(q)
and
(q{\restriction}L_{\mathbf{m},<s})\Vdash_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m},<s}}``p(s)\leq q(s)"

or

s\notin{\rm dom}(q),{\rm tr}(p(s))=\emptyset
and
q{\restriction}L_{\mathbf{m},<s}\Vdash_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m},<s}}``p(s)
is trivial, i.e.
\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{p(s)}
is constantly zero”,

(c)

{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}\models``p\leq q^{+}"
where

and

is:

(\alpha)

if

,

(\beta)

.

Remark 1.23
.

We shall use this freely.

Proof.

1) Easy but we shall elaborate.

Let
p,q\in\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}
.
If
p\leq q
then clearly
{\rm dom}(p)\subseteq{\rm dom}(q)
and
q\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}}{\text{`}\text{`}}p\in\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}"
, that is
q
is essentially above
p
.

For the other direction assume

{\rm dom}(p)\subseteq{\rm dom}(q)
but
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}\models\neg(p\leq q)
and we shall prove that
q
is not essentially above
p,

Remark 1.27
.

Used only in the proof of
\boxplus_{4.4}
inside the proof of
3.20
, so we could have used
M_{\beta},{\mathscr{E}}
from there.

Proof.

For
\ell\in\{1,2\}
let
\bar{L}_{\ell}=\langle L_{\ell,i}:i<4\rangle
be defined by:

\oplus_{1}

(a)\quad L_{\ell,0}=L_{0}
,

(b)\quad L_{\ell,1}=L_{1}
,

(c)\quad L_{\ell,2}=\{s\in L_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}:s\leq_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}t
for some
t\in L_{0}\}
,

(d)\quad L_{\ell,3}=L_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}
.

Clearly,

\oplus_{2}

(a)\quad(\mathbf{m}_{\ell},\bar{L}_{\ell})
satisfies the assumptions of
1.24
hence,

(b)\quad{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}(L_{\ell,1})\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}(L_{\ell,3})
which means
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}(L_{1})\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}
for
\ell=1,2
.

Why
\oplus_{2}
? Clearly it suffices to prove clause (a), so we just have to check clauses
(*)(a)-(f)
of
1.24
.

Clause
(*)(a)
:

By
\oplus_{1}(d),L_{\ell,3}=L_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}
hence is an initial segment of
L_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}
and by
\oplus_{1}(c),L_{\ell,2}
is an initial segment of
L_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}
which is
L_{\ell,3}
so
L_{\ell,2}\subseteq L_{\ell,3}
.

Clause
(*)(b)
:

For the first statement,
L_{\ell,1}\subseteq L_{\ell,3}
is trivial by
\oplus_{1}(d)+\oplus_{1}(b)+\boxdot(a),(b)
. The second statement says
L_{\ell,0}=L_{\ell,1}\cap L_{\ell,2}
. Now
L_{\ell,0}\subseteq L_{\ell,1}
by
\boxdot(a),(b)
of the claim and
\oplus_{1}(a),(b)
. Also
L_{\ell,0}\subseteq L_{\ell,2}
holds by
\oplus_{1}(c)
(and
\oplus_{1}(a)
). Together
L_{\ell,0}\subseteq L_{\ell,1}\cap L_{\ell,2}
; to prove
the inverse inclusion assume
s\in L_{\ell,2}\cap L_{\ell,1}
, so as
s\in L_{\ell,2}
by
\oplus_{1}(c)
there is
t\in L_{0}
such that
s\leq_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}t
. But
s\in L_{\ell,1}=L_{1}
so by
\boxdot(c)
of the claim we have
s\in L_{0}=L_{\ell,0}
as promised.

Clause
(*)(c)
:

Holds by condition
\boxdot(c)
of the claim.

Clause
(*)(d)
:

By clause
\boxdot(f)
of the claim and
\oplus_{1}(c),L_{\ell,2}
is an initial segment of
L_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}
, hence by
1.13
(e) we have
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}(L_{\ell,2})\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}={\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}(L_{\ell,3})
. By
\boxdot(e)\,{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{0}}(L_{\ell,0})\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}
; so together as

L_{\ell,0}\subseteq L_{\ell,2}
, we have
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}(L_{0})\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}(L_{\ell,2})
.

Clauses
(*)(e),(f)
:

Hold by condition
\boxdot(f)
of the claim.

So
\oplus_{2}
holds indeed. So now we deal with the other half.

Proof of
:
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L_{1})={\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}(L_{1})
.

Let
\langle s_{\alpha}:\alpha<\alpha(*)\rangle
list
L_{1}\backslash L_{0}
such that
s_{\alpha}\leq_{L_{\mathbf{m}}}s_{\beta}\Rightarrow\alpha\leq\beta
. This is possible as

is well founded.

Now,

\oplus_{3}

for
\ell=1,2
and
\alpha\leq\alpha(*)
let

be (but we can omit

(a)

(b)

(c)

for some

\oplus_{4}

,

(b)

.

[Why? Note the

\oplus_{2}

holds for
\mathbf{m}_{\ell},\bar{L}_{\ell,\alpha}
for

.]

Now by induction on
\alpha\leq\alpha(*)
we prove that:

\boxplus_{\alpha}

{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L^{*}_{\alpha,1})={\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}(L^{*}_{\alpha,1})
.

Case 1
:
\alpha=0
:

As
L^{*}_{1,\alpha,1}=L_{0}=L^{*}_{2,\alpha,1}
, clause
\boxdot(d)
of the assumption gives
\boxplus_{\alpha}
as promised.

Case 2
:
\alpha
a limit ordinal:

Easy by the definition of the iteration. That is, first, if
{\rm dom}(p)\in[L_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}]^{\leq\lambda}
then we know
p\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L^{*}_{\alpha,1})\Leftrightarrow\bigwedge\limits_{\beta<\alpha}[p{\restriction}L^{*}_{\beta,1}\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L^{*}_{\beta,1})]\Leftrightarrow\bigwedge\limits_{\beta<\alpha}[p{\restriction}L^{*}_{\beta,1}\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}(L^{*}_{\beta,1})]\Leftrightarrow p\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}(L^{*}_{\alpha,1})
;
second, for
p,q\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L^{*}_{\alpha,1})
by the definition of the order and the induction hypothesis,
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L^{*}_{\alpha,1})\models``p\leq q"

iff

\bigwedge\limits_{\beta<\alpha}[{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L^{*}_{\beta,1})\models``p{\restriction}L^{*}_{\beta,1}\leq q{\restriction}L^{*}_{\beta,1}"]

iff

\bigwedge\limits_{\beta<\alpha}[{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}(L^{*}_{\beta,1})\models``p{\restriction}L^{*}_{\beta,1}\leq q{\restriction}L^{*}_{\beta,1}"]

iff

{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}(L^{*}_{\alpha,1})\models``p\leq q"
.

So
\boxplus_{\alpha}
holds.

Case 3
:
\alpha=\beta+1
:

Clearly,

(*)_{1}

p\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L^{*}_{\alpha,1})\Leftrightarrow p\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}(L^{*}_{\alpha,1})
.

Next,

(*)_{2}

assume

and we shall prove that
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L^{*}_{\alpha,1})\models``p\leq q"
implies
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}(L^{*}_{\alpha,1})\models``p\leq q"
.

[Why? If
s_{\beta}\notin{\rm dom}(p)
this is obvious by the induction hypothesis. Hence we can assume
s_{\beta}\in{\rm dom}(p)
, so as we are assuming
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L^{*}_{\alpha,1})\models``p\leq q"
, clearly
s_{\beta}\in{\rm dom}(q)
hence
s_{\beta}\in{\rm dom}(p)\cap{\rm dom}(q)
. First, similarly
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L^{*}_{\beta,1})\models``(p{\restriction}L^{*}_{\beta,1})\leq(q{\restriction}L^{*}_{\beta,1})"
and
(q{\restriction}L^{*}_{\beta,1}){\Vdash_{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}_{1}(<s_{\beta})}}}``p(s_{\beta})\leq_{{\mathbb{Q}}_{\bar{\theta}}}q(s_{\beta})"
by the definition of
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L^{*}_{\beta,1})
. Second, as
q{\restriction}L^{*}_{\beta,1}\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L^{*}_{\beta,1})={\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}(L^{*}_{\beta,1})
and
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L^{*}_{\beta,1})\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}
by
\oplus_{4}
and
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}(L^{*}_{\beta,1})\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}
by
\oplus_{4}

and

{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L^{*}_{\beta,1})\models``p{\restriction}L^{*}_{\beta,1}\leq q{\restriction}L^{*}_{\beta,1}"
,
by the induction hypothesis
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}(L^{*}_{\beta,1})\models``p{\restriction}L^{*}_{\beta,1}\leq q{\restriction}L^{*}_{\beta,1}
. Fourth, by the last two sentence and the definition of
the order in

we have

(*)_{2}

Note that if

then
p\nleq q
, so we are done
proving
(*)_{2}
.]

(*)_{3}

[Why? Similar to the proof of
(*)_{2}

By

Remark 1.30
.

Recall if
{\mathbf{m}}
is fat, then
L\in{\mathscr{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}},t}
means
L\subseteq u_{{\mathbf{m}},t}.

Proof.

1) Easy.

2) First, concerning
(a)\Rightarrow(b)
, note that for
\gamma
large enough we
have
L^{{\rm dp}}_{{\mathbf{m}}_{\ell},\gamma}=L_{{\mathbf{m}}_{\ell}}
hence
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}(L^{{\rm dp}}_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell},\gamma})={\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}
, so clear. Second, assume (b), note that
L^{{\rm dp}}_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell},\gamma}
is an initial segment of
L_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}
hence
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}(L^{{\rm dp}}_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell},\gamma})\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}
for
\ell=1,2
by
1.13
(c), hence we have
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L^{{\rm dp}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1},\gamma})\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}
, but
\lessdot
is transitive, hence
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L^{{\rm dp}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1},\gamma})\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}
. Also
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}(L^{{\rm dp}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2},\gamma})\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}
and
L^{{\rm dp}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1},\gamma}\subseteq L^{{\rm dp}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2},\gamma}
by the definition. Hence by the definition
p\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}(L^{{\rm dp}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1},\gamma})\Rightarrow p\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}(L^{{\rm dp}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2},\gamma})
; but lastly
({\mathbb{Q}}_{1}\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}\wedge{\mathbb{Q}}_{2}\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}\wedge(\forall p)(p\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{1}\Rightarrow p\in{\mathbb{Q}}_{2})\Rightarrow{\mathbb{Q}}_{1}\lessdot{\mathbb{Q}}_{2}
so we are done.

3) Easy, as
{\mathbf{m}}\in{\mathbf{M}}_{\rm{ec}}
its suffice to find
{\mathbf{n}}
such that
{\mathbf{m}}\leq_{{\mathbf{M}}}{\mathbf{n}}
and
{\mathbf{n}}
satisfies the conclusion. So given
i_{*},t,s_{i}
such that
s_{i}<_{{\mathbf{m}}}t
(for
i<i_{*}
) we define
{\mathbf{n}}\in{\mathbf{M}}
as follows:

(a)

the set of elements of
L_{{\mathbf{n}}}
are those of
L_{{\mathbf{m}}}
and
r_{*},
a new element,

(b)

the order
<_{{\mathbf{m}}}
is defined by:
r_{1}<_{{\mathbf{m}}}r_{2}

iff

r_{1}<_{{\mathbf{m}}}r_{2}
or
r_{1}\leq_{{\mathbf{m}}}s_{i}\wedge r_{2}=r_{*}
for some
i<i_{*}
or
r_{1}=r_{\ast}\wedge t\leq_{{\mathbf{m}}}r_{2},

(c)

M_{{\mathbf{n}}}=M_{{\mathbf{m}}},

(d)

E_{{\mathbf{n}}}^{\prime}=\{(r_{1},r_{2}):(r_{1},r_{2})\in E_{{\mathbf{m}}}^{\prime}\ \text{\lx@text@underline{or}}\ r_{1}=r_{*}\wedge r_{2}\in\{s_{i}:i<i_{*}\}\cup\{t\}\ \text{\lx@text@underline{or}}\ r_{2}=r_{*}\wedge r_{1}\in\{s_{i}:i<i_{*}\}\cup\{t\}\},

(e)

u_{{\mathbf{n}},r}
is:

•

u_{{\mathbf{m}},r}

if

r\in L_{{\mathbf{m}}}\setminus\{t\},

•

u_{{\mathbf{m}},r}\cup\{r_{\ast}\}

if

r=t,

•

\{s_{i}:i<i_{*}\}

if

r=r_{*}.

(f)

{\mathscr{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}},r}
is:

•

{\mathscr{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}},r}

if

r\in L_{{\mathbf{m}}}\setminus\{t\}
,

•

{\mathscr{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}},r}\cup\{\{r_{*}\}\}

if

r=t,
except when
t\in M_{{\mathbf{m}}}^{\rm{fat}},
in which case it us
{\mathscr{P}}(u_{{\mathbf{n}},t}),

•

{\mathscr{P}}(\{s_{i}:i<i_{*}\})

if

r=r_{*}.

4) Easy by
1.16
(1)(
\beta
).

.

{\mathbf{m}}\in{\mathbf{M}}.

Crucial Claim 1.32
.

1) If
\chi=\chi^{\lambda}\geq 2^{\lambda_{2}}
(see
1.1
) and
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbf{M}_{\leq\chi}

then
for some
\mathbf{n}
we have
\mathbf{m}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{n}\in\mathbf{M}_{\chi}
and
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbf{M}_{{\rm uec}}
.

2) If in addition
{\mathbf{m}}
is bounded,
then
for some
{\mathbf{n}}
we have
{\mathbf{m}}\leq_{{\mathbf{M}}}{\mathbf{n}}\in{\mathbf{M}}_{\chi}
and
{\mathbf{n}}\in{\mathbf{M}}_{\rm{bec}}.

Proof.

Let
x=u
for part (1) and
x=b
for part (2). Let
\mathscr{X}=\mathscr{X}_{{\mathbf{m}}}=\{{\mathbf{n}}:{\mathbf{n}}
is bounded if
x=b;
and
({\mathbf{m}}{\restriction}M_{{\mathbf{m}}})\leq_{{\mathbf{M}}}{\mathbf{n}}
and
L_{{\mathbf{n}}}\setminus M_{{\mathbf{m}}}=t/E_{{\mathbf{n}}}^{\prime\prime}
for some
t,
hence
\|L_{{\mathbf{n}}}\|\leq\lambda_{2}\}.

We define a two-place relation
{\mathscr{E}}
on
{\mathscr{X}}
:

(*)_{0}

\mathbf{n}_{1}{\mathscr{E}}\mathbf{n}_{2}

iff

(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2}\in{\mathscr{X}}
and) there is an isomorphism
h
from
\mathbf{n}_{1}
onto
\mathbf{n}_{2}
over
\mathbf{m}{\restriction}M_{\mathbf{m}}
,
that is: an isomorphism from
L_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}
onto
L_{\mathbf{n}_{2}}
over
M_{\mathbf{m}}
(as partial orders) such that:

(a)

t\in L_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}\Rightarrow u_{\mathbf{n}_{2},h(t)}=\{h(s):s\in u_{\mathbf{n}_{1},t}\}
,

(b)

t\in L_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}\Rightarrow{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathbf{n}_{2},h(t)}=\{\{h(s):s\in u\}:u\in{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathbf{n}_{1},t}\}
,

(c)

s,t\in L_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}\Rightarrow(sE^{\prime}_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}t\Leftrightarrow h(s)E^{\prime}_{\mathbf{n}_{2}}h(t))
.

Clearly
{\mathscr{E}}
is an equivalence relation.

By our assumptions
\chi\geq 2^{\lambda_{2}}
and
\mathbf{n}\in{\mathscr{X}}\Rightarrow|L_{\mathbf{n}}|\leq\lambda_{2}\wedge(\forall t\in L_{{\mathbf{n}}})({\mathscr{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}},t}\subseteq[L_{{\mathbf{n}},<t}]^{\leq\lambda}),
hence recalling
\lambda_{2}=(\lambda_{2})^{\lambda}
clearly
{\mathscr{E}}
has
\leq 2^{\lambda_{2}}

.

Definition 2.2
.

Let
{\mathbb{P}}
be a forcing notion and
Y\subseteq{\mathbb{P}}
and
\chi
a regular cardinal.

1) Let
{\mathbb{L}}_{\chi}(Y)
be the set of sentences formed from
\{p:p\in{\mathbb{P}}\}
closing under the operations
\neg p
and
\bigwedge\limits_{i<\alpha}p_{i}
, for
\alpha<\chi
; so (infinitary) propositional logic.

2) For a directed
\mathbf{G}\subseteq{\mathbb{P}}
and
\psi\in{\mathbb{L}}_{\chi}(Y)
we define
the truth value
\psi[\mathbf{G}]
naturally (by induction on
\psi
starting with
p[\mathbf{G}]={\rm true}\Leftrightarrow p\in\mathbf{G}
).

3) Let
{\mathbb{L}}^{+}_{\chi}(Y,{\mathbb{P}})
, the
{\mathbb{L}}_{\chi}
-closure of
Y
for
{\mathbb{P}}
, (where
Y\subseteq{\mathbb{P}}
; if
Y={\mathbb{P}}
we may omit
Y
) be the following partial order:

\bullet

set of elements
\{\psi\in{\mathbb{L}}_{\chi}(Y,{\mathbb{P}}):\,\nVdash_{{\mathbb{P}}}``\psi[\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}]=
false”
\},

\bullet

the order
\psi_{1}\leq\psi_{2}
iff
\Vdash_{{\mathbb{P}}}
“if
\psi_{2}[\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}]=

{\mathbb{L}}^{+}_{\chi_{2}}(Y,{\mathbb{P}})
, i.e. up to the natural equivalence of elements in a quasi order,

(f)

if
Y={\mathbb{P}}
then
{\mathbb{P}}
is a dense subset of
{\mathbb{L}}^{+}_{\chi}({\mathbb{P}})
.

Proof.

Easy.
∎

Definition 2.4
.

Let
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbf{M}
.

1) For

.

Claim 2.5
.

Let
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbf{M}

.

.

\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\bar{\eta}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\bar{\eta}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\bar{\eta}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\bar{\eta}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{L}=\langle\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{s}:s\in L\rangle

The Uniqueness Claim 2.7
.

There is an isomorphism from
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}[M_{1}]
onto
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}[M_{2}]
which (recalling Definition
2.4
(1)) maps

p^{*}_{t,\eta}
to
p^{*}_{h(t),\eta}
for
t\in M_{1},\eta\in\cup\{\prod\limits_{\varepsilon<\zeta}\theta_{\varepsilon}:\zeta<\lambda\}

when
:

\boxplus

(a)\quad\mathbf{m}_{\ell}\in\mathbf{M}^{{\rm ec}}_{\infty}
for
\ell=1,2
,

(b)\quad M_{\ell}=M_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}

for
\ell=1,2
,

(c)\quad h
is an isomorphism from
\mathbf{m}_{1}{\restriction}M_{1}
onto
\mathbf{m}_{2}{\restriction}M_{2}
.

Proof.

By renaming without loss of generality
M_{1}=M_{2}
call it
M
and
h
is the identity and
L_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}\cap L_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}=M
. Let
\mathbf{m}_{0}=\mathbf{m}_{1}{\restriction}M=\mathbf{m}_{2}{\restriction}M
so
\mathbf{m}_{0}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{m}_{\ell}

for
\ell=1,2
and
L_{\mathbf{m}_{0}}=L_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}\cap L_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}
.

By
1.21
, there is
\mathbf{m}
such that
\mathbf{m}_{1}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{m}
and
\mathbf{m}_{2}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{m}
.
As
\mathbf{m}_{1},\mathbf{m}_{2}\in\mathbf{M}^{{\rm ec}}_{\infty}
by
2.5
(9)
we have
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}[M]={\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}[M]
and
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}[M]={\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}[M]
so together we get the desired conclusion.
∎

Definition 2.8
.

1) We call
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbf{M}
reduced when
L_{\mathbf{m}}=M_{\mathbf{m}}
. We call
\mathbf{m}
unary when the equivalence relation

E^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{m}}
has exactly one equivalence class.

2) For
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbf{M}
let
{\mathbb{P}}^{{\rm cor}}_{\mathbf{m}}
be
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{n}}[L_{\mathbf{m}}]
and
{\mathbb{P}}^{{\rm cor}}_{\mathbf{m}}[L]
be
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{n}}[L]
for
L\subseteq L_{\mathbf{m}}

when

\mathbf{m}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{n}\in\mathbf{M}_{{\rm ec}}
.

.

\mathbf{m}\in\mathbf{M}

.

Conclusion 2.12
.

For every ordinal
\delta_{*}
there is
\mathbf{q}=\langle{\mathbb{P}}_{\alpha},\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{\beta}:\alpha\leq\delta_{*},\beta<\delta_{*}\rangle
such that:

(A)

(a)

\langle{\mathbb{P}}_{\alpha}:\alpha\leq\delta_{*}\rangle
is
\lessdot
-increasing sequence of forcing notions,

(b)

\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{\alpha}
is a
{\mathbb{P}}_{\alpha+1}
-name of a member of
\prod\limits_{\varepsilon<\lambda}\theta_{\varepsilon}
which dominates
(\prod\limits_{\varepsilon<\lambda}\theta_{\varepsilon})^{\mathbf{V}[{\mathbb{P}}_{\alpha}]}

.

M_{{\mathbf{m}}}^{{\rm fat}}=\delta_{\ast},

.

It is easy to check that indeed

.

(2),

, the sequence
\bar{\eta}_{L}=\langle\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{\alpha}:\alpha\in L\rangle

{\mathscr{S}}_{p}\subseteq\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}}
”. More specifically it is equivalent to
{\mathscr{S}}_{p}=\{(p{\restriction}\{t\})\upharpoonleft L:t\in{\rm dom}(p)\wedge L\in{\mathscr{L}}_{t}\}

when

{\mathscr{L}}_{t}
satisfies: if
\iota<\iota_{p(s)}
then for some
L\in{\mathscr{L}}_{t},
(recalling
1.10
) we have
{\rm rang}(\bar{r}_{p(t),\iota})\subseteq L.

5) For every
p\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}},p
is equivalent to
{\mathscr{S}}^{\prime}_{p}:=\{p^{[t]}:t\in{\rm dom}(p)\}
where
p^{[t]}\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}
has domain
\{t\}
and
p(t)=({\rm tr}(p_{t}),\mathbf{B}_{p(t)}(\langle\eta_{r_{p(t)}(\zeta)}:\zeta\in w_{p(t)}\rangle)
; recall Definition
1.10
for the meaning of
{\mathbf{B}}_{p(t)}
, etc.

”.

For the other direction assume
q\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}
forces
{\mathscr{S}}_{p}\subseteq\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}\subseteq{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}
and we shall prove that
q
is compatible with
p
, this suffices, so toward contradiction assume
q,p
are incompatible.

Without loss of generality
{\rm dom}(p)\subseteq{\rm dom}(q)
and recalling
t\in{\rm dom}(p)\Rightarrow q\Vdash``p\upharpoonleft(t/E_{\mathbf{m}})\in\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}"
clearly
s\in{\rm dom}(p)\Rightarrow q\Vdash``{\rm tr}(p(s))\subseteq\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{s}"
so necessarily
s\in{\rm dom}(p)\Rightarrow{\rm tr}(p(s))\trianglelefteq{\rm tr}(q(s))
. Recalling
1.16
(6), as
p,q
are incompatible
there are
s\in{\rm dom}(p)\cap{\rm dom}(q)
and
q_{1}
such that
q{\restriction}L_{\mathbf{m},<s}\leq q_{1}\in{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}(L_{\mathbf{m},<s})
and

q_{1}\Vdash``q(s),p(s)
are incompatible in
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\bar{\theta}}"
.

As
{\rm tr}(p(s))\trianglelefteq{\rm tr}(q(s))
this implies
q_{1}\Vdash``{\rm tr}(q(s)),p(s)
are incompatible, so recalling
q\Vdash
“
{\rm tr}(p(s))\subseteq\eta_{s}
” this implies
f_{p(s)}{\restriction}\ell g({\rm tr}(q(s)))\ntrianglelefteq{\rm tr}(q(s))"
. Recalling Definition
1.10
(2)(c)(\gamma)
,

q_{1}\Vdash_{\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m},s}}

“there is
\iota<\iota(s,p)

such that
f_{p(s),\iota},{\rm tr}(q(s))
are incompatible”. Possibly increasing
q_{1}
, we can fix
\iota
. But letting

be such that
\bar{r}_{p(s),\iota}\subseteq u
this implies that
q_{1}\Vdash``(p{\restriction}\{s\})\upharpoonleft u\notin\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbf{G}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}

. However,

∎

Claim 3.5
.

Assume
\mathbf{m}
is
\mu
-wide where
\mu\geq\lambda_{0}.

1) If
f\in{\mathscr{F}}_{{\mathbf{m}},\mu}
and
X\subseteq L_{\mathbf{m}}
has cardinality

<\mu,

then
there is
g
such that:

(a)

g\in{\mathscr{F}}_{{\mathbf{m}},\infty}
and even belongs to
{\mathscr{F}}_{\mathbf{m},\mu}
,

(b)

f\subseteq g
,

(c)

{\rm dom}(g)={\rm rang}(g)
,

(d)

X\subseteq{\rm dom}(g)
.

2) If
g\in{\mathscr{F}}_{\mathbf{m},\mu}
and
{\rm dom}(g)={\rm rang}(g)

then

g^{+\mathbf{m}}=g\cup{\rm id}_{L_{\mathbf{m}}\backslash{\rm dom}(g)}
is an automorphism of
\mathbf{m}
.

3) If
f
is an automorphism of
\mathbf{m}

then
it naturally induces an automorphism
\hat{f}
of
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}(L_{\mathbf{m}})
similarly to
\hat{f}
from
(*)_{4}(b)
of the proof of
1.32
and it induces an automorphism of
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}[L_{\mathbf{m}}]
as well; abusing our notation we denote both by
\hat{f}.

4) If
f\in{\mathscr{F}}_{\mathbf{m},\mu}

then
it induces an isomorphism
\hat{f}
from
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}[{\rm dom}(f)]
onto
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}[{\rm rang}(f)]
hence (as above) from

{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}({\rm dom}(f))
onto
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}({\rm rang}(f))
.

5) If
p\in\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}

then

the set
\{t/E_{\mathbf{m}}:t\in{\rm wsupp}(p)\}
has cardinality
<\lambda
.

Proof.

1) Easy by the definition of wide in
1.31
(1) and of
{\mathscr{F}}_{\mathbf{m}}
in
3.1
(2), in particular clause (e).

2) Just read the definition of
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbf{M}
and of
f\in{\mathscr{F}}_{\mathbf{m}}
, in particular:

(a)

if
t_{1},t_{2}\in L_{\mathbf{m}}\backslash M_{\mathbf{m}}
are not
E^{\prime}_{\mathbf{m}}
-equivalent then
(t_{1}/E_{\mathbf{m}})\cap(t_{2}/E_{\mathbf{m}})=M_{\mathbf{m}}
and
\leq_{\mathbf{m}}{\restriction}(t_{1}/E_{\mathbf{m}}\cup t_{2}/E_{\mathbf{m}})
is determined by
\leq_{\mathbf{m}}{\restriction}(t_{1}/E_{\mathbf{m}}),\leq_{\mathbf{m}}{\restriction}(t_{2}/E_{\mathbf{m}}),

(b)

g{\restriction}M_{\mathbf{m}}={\rm id}_{M_{\mathbf{m}}}
.

3) Naturally by the definition.

4) Let
g\in{\mathscr{F}}
be as in part (1) and let
h=g^{+\mathbf{m}}
so an automorphism of
\mathbf{m}
which extends
g
as in part (2). So
\hat{h}
is an automorphism of
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}(L_{\mathbf{m}})
and clearly
\hat{f}=\hat{h}{\restriction}{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}({\rm dom}(f))
is as required.

5) Is clear, see
1.13
(f).
∎

Definition 3.10
.

Assume
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbf{M}
.

1) Let
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mathbf{m}}
be the set of pairs
(t,\bar{s})
such that
t\in L_{\mathbf{m}}\backslash M_{\mathbf{m}}
and
\bar{s}\in{}^{\zeta}(t/E^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{m}})
for some
\zeta<\lambda^{+}
; we may write
\bar{s}
instead of
(t,\bar{s})
as usually
\bar{s}
determines
t/E^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{m}}
, but this is the only information about
t
that matter. We could have used instead pairs
(t/E^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{m}},\bar{s})
.

2) By induction on the ordinal
\gamma
we define when
(t_{1},\bar{s}_{1}),(t_{2},\bar{s}_{2})
are
\gamma
-equivalent in
\mathbf{m}
or are
(\mathbf{m},\gamma)
-equivalent:

(a)

if
\gamma=0
,
then
letting
L_{\ell}=(M_{\mathbf{m}}\cup{\rm rang}(\bar{s}_{\ell}))
for
\ell=1,2
there is
h
such that:

(\alpha)

h
is an isomorphism from
\mathbf{m}{\restriction}L_{1}
onto
\mathbf{m}{\restriction}L_{2}
,

(\beta)

h
maps
\bar{s}_{1}
to
\bar{s}_{2}
,

(\gamma)

h{\restriction}M_{\mathbf{m}}
is the identity,

(\delta)

h
induces an isomorphism from
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}(L_{1})
onto
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}(L_{2})
(as defined in
1.5
(*)_{4}(b)
),

(\varepsilon)

moreover,
h
induces an isomorphism from
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}[L_{1}]
onto
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}[L_{2}]
, as defined in
2.7
, so
p^{*}_{t,\eta}\mapsto p^{*}_{h(t),\eta}
, see
2.4
(3),

(b)

if
\gamma=\beta+1

then
for every
\ell\in\{1,2\}
for every

\varepsilon<\lambda^{+}
and
\bar{s}^{\prime}_{\ell}\in{}^{\varepsilon}(t_{\ell}/E^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{m}})
there is
\bar{s}^{\prime}_{3-\ell}\in{}^{\varepsilon}(t_{3-\ell}/E^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{m}})
such that
(t_{1},\bar{s}_{1}\char 94\relax\bar{s}^{\prime}_{1}),(t_{2},\bar{s}_{2}\char 94\relax\bar{s}^{\prime}_{2})
are
\beta
-equivalent,

(c)

if
\gamma
is a limit ordinal
then

(t_{1},\bar{s}_{1}),(t_{2},\bar{s}_{2})
are
\beta
-equivalent for every
\beta<\gamma
.

Remark 3.11
.

1) Note above that if
\bar{s}_{\ell}
is the empty sequence
then

t_{\ell}
would not be determined by
\bar{s}_{\ell}
, still in those cases the
equivalence just means
\bar{s}_{1}=\bar{s}_{2}
.

2) We can use
t/E_{\mathbf{m}}
or
t/E^{\prime}_{\mathbf{m}}
instead of
t/E^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{m}}
as everything is over
M_{\mathbf{m}}
.

Claim 3.12
.

For
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbf{M}
and ordinal
\alpha
the number of equivalence classes of “being
(\mathbf{m},\alpha)
-equivalent” is
\leq\beth_{1+\alpha+1}(\lambda_{1})
.

Proof.

By induction on
\alpha
.

Case 1
:
\alpha=0
:

Note that the set of elements of
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}(M_{\mathbf{m}}\cup{\rm rang}(\bar{s}))
has cardinality
\leq 2^{\lambda_{1}}
(and even
\leq(\lambda_{1})^{\lambda}
) and depends just on
\mathbf{m}{\restriction}(M_{\mathbf{m}}\cup{\rm rang}(\bar{s}))
but there are
\beth_{2}(\lambda_{1})
possibilities for the quasi order on
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}(L_{1})
and even for
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}[L_{1}]
.

Case 2
:
\alpha
is a limit ordinal:

By clause (c) of Definition
3.10
, the number of
\alpha
-equivalence classes is
\leq\prod\limits_{\beta<\alpha}
(the
number of
\beta
-equivalence classes)

\leq\prod\limits_{\beta<\alpha}\beth_{1+\beta+1}(\lambda_{1})\leq(\beth_{1+\alpha+1}(\lambda_{1}))^{\beth_{1+\alpha}}=\beth_{1+\alpha+1}(\lambda_{1})
.

Case 3
:
\alpha=\beta+1
:

Clearly every
\alpha
-equivalence class can be coded as a set of
\beta
-equivalence classes hence the number of
\alpha
-equivalence
classes is
\leq 2^{\beth_{1+\beta+1}(\lambda_{1})}=\beth_{1+\beta+2}(\lambda_{1})=\beth_{1+\alpha+1}(\lambda_{1})
, as promised.
∎

Definition 3.13
.

For an ordinal
\beta
, let
{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathbf{m},\beta}
be the set of functions
f
such that for some
t^{\ell}_{i},\bar{s}^{\ell}_{i}
for
i<i(*)
and
\ell\in\{1,2\}
we have:

(a)

i(*)<\lambda^{+}
,

(b)

\langle t^{\ell}_{i}:i<i(*)\rangle
is a sequence of pairwise non-
E^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{m}}
-equivalent members of
L_{\mathbf{m}}\backslash M_{\mathbf{m}}
,

(c)

\bar{s}^{\ell}_{i}\in{}^{\zeta(i)}(t^{\ell}_{i}/E^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{m}})
where
\zeta(i)<\lambda^{+}
,

Remark 3.15
.

2) We use

in the proof, to eliminate it we can imitate the completeness theorem
29
29

29

but we give details. First as a warm up notice that (for

:

(\ast)

then

and

(

(e)

(g)

then
for some

we have:

This can be proved by induction on
\sup\{\rm{rk}(M_{{\mathbf{m}}}(s))+1:s\in{\rm dom}(r)\cap M_{{\mathbf{m}}}\}.

Let

as in the proof of

As in the proof of

\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle f$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{q(s)}{\restriction}(\lg({\rm tr}(q(s))),\lg({\rm tr}(p(s))))\leq{\rm tr}(q_{1}(s)),

Case 3
:

Having carried the induction we can define

3(D).
The main result

Here we continue §3A, §3B, and in particular prove
the main result, it does not rely on

.
Concerning §1B, we rely on it only in one point: quoting

while proving

and the beginning of Case 3 inside the proof of
3.20
, this can be avoided using §4A. We have not work out if e.g. §3D works for the fat context.

Hypothesis 3.17
.

We are in the lean context (for this subsection).

Conclusion 3.18
.

If
\beta\geq 0
and
\mathbf{m}
is wide and

f\in{\mathscr{G}}_{\mathbf{m},\beta}
and
L_{1},L_{2}
its domain and range respectively
then

from
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}(L_{1})
onto
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}(L_{2})
.

.

(3); note that this claim is not covered by Definition

(2).

\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}

So indeed

\boxplus
holds].

Claim 3.22
.

\mathbf{m}\in\mathbf{M}

Remark 3.23
.

By this we may restrict ourselves to
\mathbf{M}_{\leq\lambda_{2}}
(but then similarly in the end of §2).

Proof.

We choose
\chi
large enough and
\mathbf{m}_{*}\in\mathbf{M}_{\chi}
which is wide, belongs to
\mathbf{M}_{{\rm ec}}
and
\mathbf{m}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{m}_{*}

(1), as constructed in

).

We can choose
\mathbf{n}
such that:

(*)

\mathbf{n}\in\mathbf{M}
and
\mathbf{n}
is wide and
|L_{\mathbf{n}}|=\lambda_{2},

(b)

\mathbf{m}\leq_{{\mathbf{M}}}\mathbf{n}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{m}_{*},

(c)

(\mathbf{n},\mathbf{m}_{*})
satisfies the criterion from
3.20
, with
\mathbf{m}_{1},\mathbf{m}
there
standing for
\mathbf{n},\mathbf{m}_{*}
here.

[Why? Let
\xi=1
and recalling Definition
3.10
(1) choose
\langle(t_{\alpha},\bar{s}_{\alpha}):\alpha<\lambda_{2})\rangle

such that
(t_{\alpha},\bar{s}_{\alpha})\in{\mathscr{Y}}_{\mathbf{m}_{*}},t_{\alpha}\in L_{\mathbf{m}_{*}}\backslash M_{\mathbf{m}_{*}},\langle t_{\alpha}/E_{\mathbf{m}}:\alpha<\lambda_{2}\rangle
are pairwise distinct and for every

there are
\lambda^{+}
ordinals
\alpha<\lambda_{2}
such that
(t,\bar{s}),(t_{\alpha},\bar{s}_{\alpha})
are
\xi
-equivalent, possible by
3.12
recalling
\lambda_{2}\geq\beth_{3}(\lambda_{1})
. Let
L^{\prime}=\cup\{t_{\alpha}/E_{\mathbf{m}_{*}}:\alpha<\lambda_{2}\}\cup L_{\mathbf{m}}
and for each

let
\langle s_{t,\alpha}:\alpha<\lambda^{+}\rangle
be

and
\mathbf{m}_{*}{\restriction}(s_{t,\alpha}/E_{\mathbf{m}_{*}})
is isomorphic to

\mathbf{m}_{*}{\restriction}(t/E_{\mathbf{m}_{*}})
over
M_{\mathbf{m}}
. Let

and

. Now it is easy to check that
\mathbf{n}
is as required.]

It suffices to prove that
\mathbf{n}
belongs to
\mathbf{M}_{{\rm ec}}
, let

\mathbf{n}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{n}_{1}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{n}_{2}
.

Without loss of generality
L_{\mathbf{n}_{2}}
has cardinality
\leq 2^{\lambda_{2}}
, by the LST argument; (what is the LST argument here? let
\chi_{*}
be large enough such that
\lambda,\mathbf{m},\mathbf{m}_{*},\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2}
all belong to
{\mathscr{H}}(\chi_{*})
and let

be of cardinality

such that all the above belong to it and

. Now replace
\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2}
by their restriction to
\mathfrak{A}
).

Now as
\mathbf{m}_{*}
is very wide and full without loss of generality
\mathbf{n}_{2}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{m}_{*}
. Now
(\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{m}_{*})

3.20
hence
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{*}}
.

Also the pair
(\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathbf{m}_{*})
satisfies the criterion from
3.20
looking at the criterion. Hence by
3.20
we have
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{n}_{2}}\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{*}}
.

As
\mathbf{n}_{1}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{n}_{2}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{m}_{*}
from the last two sentences it easily follows that
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{n}_{2}}
, so we are done.
∎

Discussion 3.24
.

In what way does this proof help? Will it not be simpler to omit in Definition
1.10
clause (c) the
\iota_{p(s)},\mathbf{B}_{p(s),\iota}
, etc.?

In this case in
3.1
we cannot define the projection directly hence we should look for projection as in general forcing, but then we
run into problems of absoluteness. More specifically,
3.20
seems to be problematic; anyhow this does not matter.

Definition 3.25
.

For
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbf{M}
and
M
is a subset of
M_{{\mathbf{m}}}
so of cardinality
\leq\lambda_{1}
we define
\mathbf{n}:=\mathbf{m}\langle M\rangle\in\mathbf{M}
as follows:

(a)

L_{\mathbf{n}}=L_{\mathbf{m}}
even as a partial order,

(b)

\bar{u}_{\mathbf{n}}=\bar{u}_{\mathbf{m}}
and
\bar{{\mathscr{P}}}_{\mathbf{n}}=\bar{{\mathscr{P}}}_{\mathbf{m}}
,

(c)

M_{\mathbf{n}}=M
; yes
M
not
M_{\mathbf{m}}
!

(d)

E^{\prime}_{\mathbf{n}}=\{(s,t):s,t\in L_{\mathbf{m},}
and
\{s,t\}\nsubseteq M\}.

Claim 3.26
.

Assume
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbf{M}_{\leq\lambda_{2}}
and
M
is a subset of
M_{\mathbf{m}}
.

1)
\mathbf{n}:=\mathbf{m}\langle M\rangle
indeed belongs to
\mathbf{M}
and is equivalent to
\mathbf{m}
hence
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}}(L_{\mathbf{m}})={\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{n}}(L_{\mathbf{m}})
i.e.
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}={\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}.

2) If
\mathbf{n}=\mathbf{m}\langle M\rangle\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{n}_{1}

then
for some
\mathbf{m}_{1}
we have
\mathbf{m}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{m}_{1}
and
\mathbf{m}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{1}
are equivalent.

3) If
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbf{M}_{{\rm ec}}
and
\mathbf{n}=\mathbf{m}\langle M\rangle

then

\mathbf{n}\in\mathbf{M}_{{\rm ec}}
.

4) If
{\mathbf{m}}\in{\mathbf{M}}_{{\rm wec}}
and
{\mathbf{n}}={\mathbf{m}}\langle M\rangle

then

{\mathbf{n}}\in{\mathbf{M}}_{{\rm wec}}.

Proof.

1) Check, noting that
t\in L_{\mathbf{n}}\backslash M_{\mathbf{n}}\Rightarrow t\in L_{\mathbf{m}}\backslash M\Rightarrow|t/E^{\prime}_{\mathbf{n}}|\leq|L_{\mathbf{n}}|=|L_{\mathbf{m}}|\leq\lambda_{2}
and
|M_{\mathbf{m}}|=|M|\leq|M_{\mathbf{m}}|\leq\lambda_{1}
, (in fact, here
M\subseteq M_{\mathbf{m}}
is not necessary, only
``M
has cardinality
\leq\lambda_{1}"
).

2) Given such
\mathbf{n}_{1}
we now define
\mathbf{m}_{1}\in\mathbf{M}
by:

(*)_{1}

(a)

L_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}=L_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}
,

(b)

and
\bar{{\mathscr{P}}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}=\bar{{\mathscr{P}}}_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}
,

(c)

M_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}=M_{\mathbf{m}}
,

(d)

E^{\prime}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}=\{(s,t):sE^{\prime}_{\mathbf{m}}t

or

\{s,t\}\nsubseteq L_{\mathbf{m}}
but
\{s,t\}\subseteq L_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}
and
sE^{\prime}_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}t\}
.

Clearly:

(*)_{2}

(a)

\langle M_{\mathbf{m}}\rangle\char 94\relax\langle s/E^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{m}}:s\in L_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}\backslash M_{\mathbf{m}}\rangle\char 94\relax\langle t/E^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}:t\in L_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}\backslash L_{\mathbf{n}}\rangle
is a partition of
L_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}=L_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}
,

(b)

and
s,t\notin M_{\mathbf{m}}\}
is an equivalence relation, its equivalence classes being the sets listed in clause (a) except
M_{\mathbf{m}}
,

(c)

\mathbf{m}_{1}
satisfies clause
(e)(\gamma)
of Definition
1.5
.

(*)_{3}

(a)

if
s\in L_{\mathbf{m}}\backslash M_{\mathbf{m}}
then:

(\alpha)

,

s/E^{\prime}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}=s/E^{\prime}_{\mathbf{m}}
,

(\gamma)

,

.

(b)

if

then:

(\alpha)

,

,

,

.

,

and easily,

(*)_{4}

indeed
\mathbf{m}_{1}\in\mathbf{M}
,

,

So we are done.

3) Assume
\mathbf{n}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{n}_{1}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{n}_{2}
, as in the proof of part (2) there are
\mathbf{m}_{1},\mathbf{m}_{2}
such that

and

. As

we have

but this means
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{n}_{2}}
, as required.

4) Similarly because
{\mathbf{m}}\in{\mathbf{M}}_{\rm{wbd}}\Rightarrow{\mathbf{m}}\langle M\rangle\in{\mathbf{M}}_{\rm{wbd}}
. ∎

Conclusion 3.27
.

1) If
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbf{M},M
is a subset of
M_{\mathbf{m}}
and
\mathbf{n}=\mathbf{m}{\restriction}M

then

{\mathbb{P}}^{{\rm cor}}_{\mathbf{n}}\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}^{{\rm cor}}_{\mathbf{m}}
.

2) If
\mathbf{m}_{\ell}\in\mathbf{M}
and
M_{\ell}
is a subset of
M_{\mathbf{m}_{\ell}}
for
\ell=1,2
and
h
is an isomorphism from
\mathbf{m}_{1}{\restriction}M_{1}
onto
\mathbf{m}_{2}{\restriction}M_{2}

then

h
induces an isomorphism from
{\mathbb{P}}^{{\rm cor}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}[M_{1}]
onto
{\mathbb{P}}^{{\rm cor}}_{\mathbf{m}_{2}}[M_{2}]
.

Proof.

1) Without loss of generality
\mathbf{m}\in\mathbf{M}_{\leq\lambda_{2}}
; (why? because trivially
\mathbf{n}\in\mathbf{M}_{\leq\lambda_{1}}
and letting
\mathbf{m}_{1}=\mathbf{m}{\restriction}M_{\mathbf{m}}
we have
\mathbf{m}_{1}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{m}
and
\mathbb{P}^{{\rm cor}}_{\mathbf{m}_{1}}=\mathbb{P}^{{\rm cor}}_{\mathbf{m}}[M_{\mathbf{m}}]
and
\mathbf{n}=\mathbf{m}_{1}{\restriction}M_{\mathbf{m}}
). By
3.22
there is
\mathbf{m}_{*}\in\mathbf{M}^{{\rm ec}}_{\lambda_{2}}
such that
\mathbf{m}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{m}_{*}
hence
by
2.10
(2)
{\mathbb{P}}^{{\rm cor}}_{\mathbf{m}}={\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{*}}[M_{\mathbf{m}}]
.

Let
\mathbf{n}_{*}=\mathbf{m}_{*}\langle M\rangle
, see Definition
3.25
, so
\mathbf{n}_{*}{\restriction}M=\mathbf{n}
and by
3.26
(3)
we have
\mathbf{n}_{*}\in\mathbf{M}_{{\rm ec}}
, hence
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{n}_{*}}[M_{\mathbf{n}}]={\mathbb{P}}^{{\rm cor}}_{\mathbf{n}}
. But
\mathbf{n}_{*},\mathbf{m}_{*}
are equivalent, hence
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{n}_{*}}={\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{*}}
hence
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{n}_{*}}[L]={\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{*}}[L]
for every
L\subseteq L_{\mathbf{m}_{*}}
hence by
2.10
(3)

{\mathbb{P}}^{{\rm cor}}_{\mathbf{n}}={\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{n}_{*}}[M_{\mathbf{n}}]\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{n}_{*}}[M_{\mathbf{m}}]={\mathbb{P}}_{\mathbf{m}_{*}}[M_{\mathbf{m}}]={\mathbb{P}}^{{\rm cor}}_{\mathbf{m}}
. So the conclusion holds.

2) Easy, too.
∎
