L
is good then
L^{+}=\{s:\text{there is no}\ t\in M_{{\mathbf{m}}}\setminus L\ \text{such that}\ t\leq_{{\mathbf{m}}}s\}
is good.

Proof.

Notice that 1), 2) and 3) are straightforward. Concerning part (2) the reader may wonder: how do you define
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}(L)
not using parameters if, say, multiple such
t_{\ast}
’s exists?. The answer is by
4.2
(3) clause (c); that is, each
t\in M_{{\mathbf{m}}}
is definable without parameters.

4) The point is that we do not like to induct on
\rm{dp}(s,L_{{\mathbf{m}}})
just on
\rm{dp}(t,M_{{\mathbf{m}}}).
Note that the clauses on
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[L^{+}]
follows by those on
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}(L^{+}).
What we do is noting:

\oplus

for
p,q\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}(L^{+}),p\leq q
iff:

(a)

p{\restriction}L\leq_{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}(L)}q{\restriction}L,

(b)

if
s\in{\rm dom}(p)\setminus L

then
necessarily
s\in L^{+}\setminus L
and
s/E_{{\mathbf{m}}}^{\prime\prime}
appears in
\langle t_{\alpha}/E_{{\mathbf{m}}}:\alpha<\alpha_{{\mathbf{m}}}\rangle
and,

\bullet

{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[L\cup(s/E_{{\mathbf{m}}})]\models p{\restriction}(L\cup(L^{+}\cap s/E_{{\mathbf{m}}}))\subseteq q{\restriction}(L\cup(L^{+}\cap s/E_{{\mathbf{m}}})).

[Why? Just think.]

Recalling
4.2
(3)(A)(d) it suffice to prove that:

(\ast)

Assume
s\in L^{+}\setminus L,p,q\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}
and
{\rm dom}(p)\subseteq{\rm dom}(q)\subseteq L\cup(s/E_{{\mathbf{m}}}^{\prime})

then

{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}\models
“
p\leq q
iff (a)
+
(b)”, where:

(a)

(p{\restriction}L)\leq_{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}(L)}(q{\restriction}L),

(b)

for some
\psi\in{\mathbb{L}}_{\lambda^{+}}[Y_{s/E_{{\mathbf{m}}}^{\prime}\cap L}]
we have:

\bullet_{1}

{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[L_{{\mathbf{m}}}]\models\psi\subseteq q,

\bullet_{2}

\psi\wedge q\wedge\neg p\notin{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[L^{+}\cap(s/E_{{\mathbf{m}}}^{\prime})].

[Why
(\ast)
holds? As in §3C.]
∎

Claim 4.7
.

1)
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}
is
\mathbb{L}_{\lambda^{+}_{1},\lambda^{+}_{1}}
-interpretable in
{\rm md}(\mathbf{m}).

2) We have
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}\lessdot\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{n}}

when
:

(a)

\mathbf{m}\leq_{\mathbf{M}}\mathbf{n},

(b)

for every
\zeta<\lambda^{+}_{1}
and
t\in L_{{\mathbf{n}}}\setminus L_{\mathbf{m}}
there are at least
\lambda^{+}_{1}
elements
s\in L_{{\mathbf{m}}}\setminus M_{{\mathbf{m}}}
such that, recalling
4.2
(3), the models
{\rm md}(\mathbf{n}){\restriction}(t/E_{{\mathbf{n}}}),\,{\rm md}(\mathbf{n}){\restriction}(s/E_{n})
are
\mathbb{L}_{\beth^{+}_{\zeta},\beth^{+}_{\zeta}}
-equivalent.

3) If
{\mathbf{n}}\in{\mathbf{M}}_{\rm{ec}}
is wide and full,
A\subseteq L_{{\mathbf{n}}}
has cardinality
\leq\beth_{\lambda_{1}^{+}}

then
there is
{\mathbf{m}}
such that:

(a)

{\mathbf{m}}\leq_{{\mathbf{M}}}{\mathbf{n}},

(b)

L_{{\mathbf{m}}}
has cardinality
\leq\beth_{\lambda_{1}^{+}},

(c)

{\mathbf{m}}\in{\mathbf{M}}_{\rm{ec}}.

4) Similarly to part (3) for
{\mathbf{M}}_{\rm{bec}}.

Proof.

1) Let
\langle s_{\zeta}:\zeta<\zeta_{\ast}\rangle
lists the elements of
M_{{\mathbf{m}}}
such that
s_{{\varepsilon}}<_{M_{{\mathbf{m}}}}s_{{\varepsilon}}\Rightarrow{\varepsilon}<\zeta:
exists as
L_{{\mathbf{m}}}
is a (possibly partial) well order. Clearly
\zeta_{\ast}<\|M_{{\mathbf{m}}}\|^{+}\leq\lambda_{1}^{+}.
We define
L_{\zeta}
for
\zeta\leq 2\zeta_{\ast}+1
as follows:

•

if
{\varepsilon}\leq\zeta_{\ast},
then
L_{2{\varepsilon}}=\{t\in L_{{\mathbf{m}}}:
for some
\zeta<{\varepsilon}
we have
t\leq_{L_{{\mathbf{m}}}}s_{\zeta}\},

•

for
{\varepsilon}\leq\zeta_{\ast}
we let
L_{2{\varepsilon}+1}=L_{2{\varepsilon}}\cup\{t\in L_{{\mathbf{m}}}:
if
\zeta\in[{\varepsilon},\zeta_{\ast})
then
s_{\zeta}\nleq t

or
for some
\xi<{\varepsilon}
we have
(\forall\zeta\in[\xi,\zeta_{\ast}))(s_{\zeta}\nleq t)\}.

Pedantically, the models
\langle{\rm md}(\mathbf{m}){\restriction}A_{\mathbf{m},\alpha}:\alpha<\alpha_{\mathbf{m}}\rangle
are not pairwise disjoint but the common part consists of
\lambda_{1}
individual constants
hence
this does not matter.

Clearly
L_{2\zeta_{\ast}+1}=L_{{\mathbf{m}}},L_{{\varepsilon}}
is a definable initial segment of
L_{{\mathbf{m}}}.
By Definition
4.5
it suffice to prove that
L_{{\mathbf{m}}}
is good.

Now we prove by induction on
{\varepsilon}
that
L_{{\varepsilon}}
is good, so for
{\varepsilon}=2\zeta_{\ast}+1
we get the desired conclusion.

For
\zeta=0,
this holds by
4.6
(1).

For
\zeta=2{\varepsilon}+1
we have
L_{\zeta}\setminus L_{2{\varepsilon}}=\{s_{{\varepsilon}}\}
and
L_{{\mathbf{m}}(<s_{\alpha})}\subseteq L_{2{\varepsilon}};
hence by
4.6
(2) we are done.

For
\zeta=2{\varepsilon}+2
we apply
4.6
(4).

Lastly, for
\zeta
limit we apply
4.6
(3). Together we are done.

2) By part (1) and the addition theorem, (best formulated for the intermediate logic
\mathbb{L}_{\infty,\lambda^{+}_{1},\zeta}
for
\zeta<\lambda^{+}_{1})
, see
[
Dic85
]
).

3), 4) As in the proof of
4.8
below.
∎

.

\lambda^{+}
-directed,

is cofinal in

then

\Vdash_{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}}
“
\{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{s}:s\in M\}

General
{\mathbf{m}}
’s

.

Definition 4.10
.

Assume
\mathbf{m}
is
\lambda_{0}
-wide. Let
\mathbb{P}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{m}}=\mathbb{P}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{m}}[M_{\mathbf{m}}]
be the forcing notion
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}[M_{\mathbf{m}}]
restricted to the set of
\psi\in\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}[M_{\mathbf{m}}]
such that there is
p\in\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}(L_{\mathbf{m}})
witnessing it, which means that (it is the projection of
p
into
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}[M_{\mathbf{m}}]
, that is):

\bullet_{1}

the condition
\psi
is smaller or equal to
p
in the forcing notion
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}[L_{\mathbf{m}}],

\bullet_{2}

if
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}[M_{\mathbf{m}}]\models``\psi\leq\varphi"

then

\varphi,p
are compatible in the forcing notion
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}[L_{\mathbf{m}}].

Claim 4.11
.

Assume
\mathbf{m}
to be
\lambda_{0}
-wide.

1)
\mathbb{P}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{m}}
is a dense subset of
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}[M_{\mathbf{m}}]
, hence
\mathbb{P}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{m}}\lessdot\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}[L_{\mathbf{m}}]
.

2) If
L
is an initial segment of
L_{{\mathbf{m}}}
and
{\mathbf{n}}={\mathbf{m}}{\restriction}L,

then

\mathbb{P}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{n}}=\mathbb{P}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{m}}\cap\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{n}}[M_{\mathbf{n}}]
.

3) If
L
is a
\lambda_{0}
-wide initial segment of
L_{\mathbf{m}}
, and
\mathbf{n}=\mathbf{m}\upharpoonright L,

then
:

(a)

\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{n}}[M_{\mathbf{n}}]\lessdot\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{n}}[L_{\mathbf{n}}]
and
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{n}}[M_{\mathbf{n}}]=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}[M_{\mathbf{n}}]\lessdot\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}[M_{\mathbf{m}}]
,

(b)

if
p_{1}\in\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{n}}(L_{\mathbf{n}})

then
there is
\psi\in\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{n}}[M_{\mathbf{n}}]
satisfying:

(
\alpha
)

\psi\leq p_{1}\in\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{n}}[L_{\mathbf{n}}]
,

(
\beta
)

if
\psi\leq\varphi\in\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{n}}[M_{\mathbf{n}}]
then
p_{1},\varphi
are compatible in
\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{n}}[L_{\mathbf{n}}]
,

(
\gamma
)

\psi
being witnessed by
p_{1}
, (see Definition
4.10
this follows).

Proof.

1) Let
\varphi\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[M_{{\mathbf{m}}}]
and we should find
\psi\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}^{{\dagger}}
above it, this suffice. Clearly there is
p_{1}\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}
such that
\varphi\leq p_{1},
that is
p_{1}\Vdash
“
\varphi\in\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[L_{{\mathbf{m}}}]}}
”. Now let
\langle\psi_{i}:i<i_{*}\rangle
be a maximal anti-chain of members of
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}[M_{{\mathbf{n}}}]
which are incompatible with
p_{1}
in
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[L_{{\mathbf{m}}}].
Clearly
i_{*}<\lambda^{+}
hence without loss of generality
i_{*}\leq\lambda
and let
\psi=\bigwedge_{i<i_{*}}\neg\psi_{i}.
Clearly
p_{1}
witnesses
\psi\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}^{{\dagger}}
hence
\varphi\leq\psi,
see more details in the proof of
4.11
(3).

2) Trivially
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}^{\dagger}\subseteq{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}[M_{{\mathbf{n}}}],
so it suffice assume
\psi\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}[M_{{\mathbf{n}}}]
and prove
\psi\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}^{\dagger}\Leftrightarrow\psi\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}^{\dagger}.

First assume
\psi\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}^{\dagger}
is witnessed by
p\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}
and we shall prove that
p
witness
\psi\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}^{\dagger};
we have to check the two conditions
\bullet_{1}+\bullet_{2}
of Definition
4.10
. Now clearly
p\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}
and
\psi\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[M_{{\mathbf{m}}}]
(the second because
\psi\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[M_{{\mathbf{m}}}]
and
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}
and
M_{{\mathbf{n}}}\subseteq M_{{\mathbf{m}}},
hence
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[M_{{\mathbf{n}}}]\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[M_{{\mathbf{m}}}]
). Also
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}[L_{{\mathbf{n}}}]\models\psi\leq p
but
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}[L_{{\mathbf{n}}}]\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[L_{{\mathbf{m}}}]
hence
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[L_{{\mathbf{m}}}]\models\psi\leq p.
So in Definition
4.10
condition
\bullet_{1}
holds; for proving condition
\bullet_{2},
assume that
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[M_{{\mathbf{m}}}]\models
“
\psi\leq\varphi
” hence, by part (1), we can find
q\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}
and
\vartheta\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[M_{{\mathbf{m}}}]
which is witnessed by
q
such that
\vartheta
is above
\varphi.
Without loss of generality,
{\rm dom}(q)\cap{\rm dom}(p)\subseteq M_{{\mathbf{m}}}
and let
q_{1}=q{\restriction}L,
now
q_{1},\psi
are compatible in
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[L_{{\mathbf{m}}}],
hence in
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}[L_{{\mathbf{n}}}],
also
{\rm dom}(q_{1})\cap{\rm dom}(p)
is included in
L=L_{{\mathbf{n}}}
and is included in
{\rm dom}(q)\cap{\rm dom}(p)
which is included in
M_{{\mathbf{m}}};
together
{\rm dom}(q_{1})\cap{\rm dom}(p)\subseteq L_{{\mathbf{n}}}\cap M_{{\mathbf{m}}}=M_{{\mathbf{n}}}.
Therefore by
3.14
(1),
p,q_{1}
are compatible in
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}(L_{{\mathbf{m}}}),
hence in
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}(L_{{\mathbf{n}}})
, so let
r\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}
be a common upper bound. As
q{\restriction}L\leq_{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}}r,
clearly
r,q
has a common upper bound
r_{1}
(in
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}
) and so
r_{1}
is a common upper bound of
\varphi,p.

So we are done proving one implication (the “first” above) and the second is easier: if
p\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}
witness
\psi\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}^{\dagger},
then
p{\restriction}L
witness
\psi\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}^{\dagger}.

3) Why?

Clause (a)
:

The first clause is obvious, the second recalling
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}
it is clear.

Clause (b)
:

In
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}[M_{{\mathbf{n}}}]
let
\bar{\psi}=\langle\psi_{i}:i<i_{*}\rangle,\psi
be as in the proof of part (1) for
p_{1}
(and
{\mathbf{n}}
).

Now,

(*)

\bar{\psi}
is maximal also for
{\mathbf{m}},p_{1}.

Why
(*)
holds? It means: if
\vartheta\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[M_{{\mathbf{m}}}]
is incompatible with
p_{1}
in
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[L_{{\mathbf{m}}}]
then
\vartheta
is compatible with some
\psi_{i}
(
i<i_{*}
) in
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[L_{{\mathbf{m}}}].
But if
\vartheta
is a counterexample then there is
p_{2}\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}(L_{{\mathbf{m}}})
above
\vartheta,
so
p_{2}
is incompatible with
\psi_{i}
for
i<i_{*}
and with
p_{1}.
Let
q_{2}\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}(L_{{\mathbf{n}}})
be above
p_{2}{\restriction}L_{{\mathbf{n}}}
and decide “does
\psi\in\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[L_{{\mathbf{m}}}]}
”. As
q_{2},p_{2}
are compatible necessarily
q_{2}\Vdash
“
\psi\in\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[L_{{\mathbf{n}}}]}
” hence
q_{2}\Vdash_{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}[L_{{\mathbf{n}}}]}
“
\psi\in\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle{\mathbf{G}}$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}[L_{{\mathbf{n}}}]}
”. Let
\vartheta_{*}\in{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}[M_{{\mathbf{n}}}]
be witnessed by
q_{2}
(exists by part (1)) so
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}[L_{{\mathbf{n}}}]\models
“
\psi\leq\vartheta_{*}
”. Also without loss of generality

are incompatible in

so

contradicts the maximality of

∎

is a common upper bound of
\{p_{i}:i<i_{*}\}

then

q
witnesses
\psi\in\mathbb{P}^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{m}}[M_{\mathbf{m}}]
.

Proof.

1) Clearly
q\in\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}.
Also if
r\in\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}
is a common upper bound of
\{p_{i}:i<i_{*}\}
clearly
q\leq r\in\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{m}}
.

2) Easy and will not be used.
∎

4(C).
Nicely existentially closed

Discussion 4.17
.

1) In the main case we have
M\subseteq M_{{\mathbf{m}}}
cofinal in
M_{{\mathbf{m}}}
and
{\mathbf{m}}{\restriction}M\cong{\mathbf{m}}{\restriction}M_{{\mathbf{m}}}.
In §4A we proved that if
{\mathbf{m}}\in M_{\rm{ec}}
then
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[M]\cong{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[M_{{\mathbf{m}}}]
even in the general case.

Our main aim is to prove more; e.g.

(*)

(a)

for
{\mathbf{m}},M
as above, there is
{\mathbf{n}}\in{\mathbf{M}}
such that
{\mathbf{n}}={\mathbf{m}}{\restriction}L_{{\mathbf{n}}},M_{{\mathbf{n}}}=M
and
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}\lessdot{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}},

(b)

moreover there is
{\mathbf{n}}
such that

and
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[L_{{\mathbf{m}}}]
is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{n}}}[L_{{\mathbf{n}}}]
over
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}[M].

2) In the main case (
{\mathbf{m}}\in{\mathbf{M}}_{{\rm ec}}
is strongly by
\lambda^{+}
-directed even in the general case),

\bullet

\Vdash_{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{m}}}}
“
\{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\textstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\eta$\cr\vbox to0.86108pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\tilde{\mkern-3.0mu}\mkern 3.0mu}{}$}\vss}\crcr}}}_{s}:s\in M\}
is cofinal in
(\Pi_{{\varepsilon}<\lambda}\theta_{{\varepsilon}},<_{J_{\lambda}^{\rm{bd}}})
”.

From Definition
4.18
we shall use
L^{\prime}=\bigcup\rm{cmp}(M,{\mathbf{m}})
defined below.

Earlier in §3D we doctored
{\mathbf{m}}\in{\mathbf{M}}_{\leq\lambda_{2}}
to an equivalent
{\mathbf{n}}
such that
M_{{\mathbf{n}}}=M_{{\mathbf{m}}},\,E^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathbf{n}}}
has one equivalent class “glueing” together all

Here things are more elaborated. First, in

we define the set
\rm{cmp}(M,{\mathbf{m}})
of the

for which

is enough and then in

doctor
{\mathbf{m}}
to an equivalent
{\mathbf{n}}

Later we can treat

as earlier.

Definition 4.18
.

Assume
{\mathbf{m}}\in{\mathbf{M}}
and
M\subseteq M_{{\mathbf{m}}}.

1) Let
\rm{cmp}(M,{\mathbf{m}})
be the set of
L
such that for some
t\in L_{{\mathbf{m}}}\setminus M_{{\mathbf{m}}},
we have:

(a)

L=t/E_{{\mathbf{m}}}^{\prime}=\{s\in L_{{\mathbf{m}}}:sE_{{\mathbf{m}}}^{\prime}t\},

(b)

L\cap M_{{\mathbf{m}}}\subseteq M.

2) Let
\rm{cmp}^{+}(M,{\mathbf{m}})=\rm{cmp}(M,{\mathbf{m}})\cup\{M\}.

3) For
t_{*}\in M
let
\rm{cmp}(t_{*},M,{\mathbf{m}})
be the set of
L\in\rm{cmp}(M,{\mathbf{m}})
such that
L\subseteq L_{{\mathbf{m}}(\leq t_{*})}
and similarly
\rm{cmp}^{+}(t_{*},M,{\mathbf{m}})={\rm cmp}(t_{\ast},M,{\mathbf{m}})\cup(M_{{\mathbf{m}}(\leq t_{\ast})}\cap M).

Definition 4.19
.

Assume
{\mathbf{m}}\in{\mathbf{M}},M\subseteq M_{{\mathbf{m}}}

\mathscr{L}\subseteq\rm{cmp}(M,{\mathbf{m}}).

{\mathbf{M}}

Proof.

1) Let

so
\alpha_{*}<\lambda_{2}
such that

We choose by induction on

and if
\alpha<\alpha_{*},
also
{\mathbf{n}}_{\alpha}^{0},{\mathbf{n}}_{\alpha}^{1},{\mathscr{L}}_{\alpha}

Why can we carry the induction?

First, arriving to
\alpha
we choose
{\mathbf{m}}_{\alpha}
as follows:

\bullet

if
\alpha=0
let
{\mathbf{m}}_{\alpha}={\mathbf{m}}
(so
(*)(c)
holds).

•

\alpha

•

if

so
{\mathbf{n}}_{\beta}^{0},{\mathbf{n}}_{\beta}^{1}
have been chosen then choose

by

(4) and

and
{\mathbf{m}}_{\alpha}
has been defined we choose

as

holds.

So we have carried the induction.

(*)_{1}

Let
{\mathbf{n}}={\mathbf{m}}_{\alpha_{*}}
so
{\mathbf{n}}\in{\mathbf{M}}_{\leq\lambda_{2}}
and
{\mathbf{m}}={\mathbf{n}}_{0}\leq_{{\mathbf{M}}}{\mathbf{n}}.

So clause
\boxplus(a)
holds. Why
{\mathbf{n}}\in{\mathbf{M}}_{\rm{bec}}?
(i.e. clause
\boxplus(b)
). As clearly

is strongly
(<\lambda^{+})
-directed, by

Why clauses

so clearly.

(*)_{2}

\bullet_{1}

(1),

(*)_{3}

for

So we are done proving

Now
\boxplus(c)
is just a choice, so we are left with

which says

but it follows by
(*)_{2}
and
(*)_{3}.

2) We can find
{\mathbf{n}}^{+}
such that:

(*)

(a)

(
{\mathbf{n}}
is from part (1)),
